    "# Calculate the net \"Trade Balance\" for each country (Total Exports minus Total Imports). \n",
    "# Which five countries have the highest positive trade balance (net exporters), and which \n",
    "# five have the most negative trade balance (net importers)?\n",
    "\n",
    "trade = pd.merge(\n",
    "    df_exports[[\"Country\", \"Total_export\"]],\n",
    "    df_imports[[\"Country\", \"Total_import\"]],\n",
    "    on=\"Country\",\n",
    "    how=\"outer\",                      \n",
    ")\n",
    "trade[\"Total_export\"] = trade[\"Total_export\"].fillna(0)\n",
    "trade[\"Total_import\"] = trade[\"Total_import\"].fillna(0)\n",
    "trade[\"trade_balance\"] = trade[\"Total_export\"] - trade[\"Total_import\"]\n",
    "top_exporters = trade.nlargest(5, \"trade_balance\")\n",
    "top_importers = trade.nsmallest(5, \"trade_balance\")\n",
    "print(\"TOP EXPORTERS\\n\", top_exporters)\n",
    "print(\"\\nTOP IMPORTERS\\n\", top_importers)"

    "# Group the production data by specific coffee type. What is the overall global production \n",
    "# ratio between the different types across the entire dataset timeline?\n",
    "\n",
    "year_cols = df_production.drop(columns=[\"Country\", \"Coffee type\", \"Total_production\"]).columns\n",

   "source": [
    "# Identify any countries that show records of exporting coffee but have zero recorded coffee \n",
    "# production. How might you explain this anomaly economically?\n",
    "\n",
    "export_year_cols = df_exports.drop(columns=[\"Country\", \"Total_export\"]).columns\n",
    "prod_year_cols = df_production.drop(columns=[\"Country\", \"Coffee type\", \"Total_production\"]).columns\n",
    "\n",
    "pairs = list(zip(export_year_cols, prod_year_cols))\n",
    "hits = []\n",
    "for country in df_exports[\"Country\"]:\n",
    "    for ey, py in pairs:\n",
    "        ev = df_exports.set_index(\"Country\").loc[country, ey]\n",
    "        pv = df_production.set_index(\"Country\").loc[country, py]\n",
    "        if pd.notna(ev) and ev > 0 and (pd.isna(pv) or pv == 0):\n",
    "            hits.append({\"Country\": country, \"Year\": ey,\n",
    "                         \"Export\": ev, \"Production\": pv})\n",
    "anomaly_years = pd.DataFrame(hits)\n",
    "summary = (anomaly_years\n",
    "           .groupby(\"Country\")\n",
    "           .agg(affected_years=(\"Year\", \"count\"),\n",
    "                exported=(\"Export\", \"sum\"))\n",
    "           .sort_values(\"exported\", ascending=False))\n",
    "\n",
    "summary\n"
   ]
  },
  {
   "cell_type": "markdown",
   "id": "4f825642",
   "metadata": {},
   "source": [
    "Lao People's Democratic Republic, Yemen, Equatorial Guinea, Gabon, and Guyana are countries \n",
    "that show records of exports with zero recorded production. I think this anomaly exists\n",
    "because these countries act as 'intermediaries' to ship coffee from other countries."
   ]

   "source": [
    "decades = {\"1990s\": [str(y) for y in range(1990, 2000)],\n",
    "           \"2000s\": [str(y) for y in range(2000, 2010)],\n",
    "           \"2010s\": [str(y) for y in range(2010, 2020)]}\n",
    "colors  = {\"1990s\": \"#8c510a\", \"2000s\": \"#d8b365\", \"2010s\": \"#5ab4ac\"}\n",
    "\n",
    "exp_years = df_exports.drop(columns=[\"Country\", \"Total_export\"]).columns\n",
    "imp_years = df_imports.drop(columns=[\"Country\"]).columns \n",
    "\n",
    "exp_total = df_exports.set_index(\"Country\")[exp_years].sum(axis=1)\n",
    "imp_total = df_imports.set_index(\"Country\")[imp_years].sum(axis=1)\n",
    "\n",
    "top_exp = exp_total.nlargest(5).index \n",
    "top_imp = imp_total.nlargest(5).index\n",
    "\n",
    "def decade_stack(df, countries):\n",
    "    idx = df.set_index(\"Country\").loc[countries]\n",
    "    return pd.DataFrame({d: idx[cols].sum(axis=1) for d, cols in decades.items()}, index=countries)\n",
    "\n",
    "fig, (ax1, ax2) = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(12, 5), sharex=True)\n",
    "for ax, df, countries, title in [(ax1, df_exports, top_exp, \"Top 5 Net Exporters\"),\n",
    "                                 (ax2, df_imports, top_imp, \"Top 5 Net Importers\")]:\n",
    "    stack, bottom = decade_stack(df, countries), None\n",
    "    order = stack.index[::-1]                       # largest country at top\n",
    "    for decade in decades:                         # ← the stacking: left= accumulates\n",
    "        vals = stack.loc[order, decade]\n",
    "        ax.barh(order, vals, left=bottom, color=colors[decade], label=decade)\n",
    "        bottom = vals if bottom is None else bottom + vals\n",
    "    ax.set_title(title); ax.set_xlabel(\"Bags (30-year total)\")\n",
    "ax1.legend(title=\"Decade\")\n",
    "fig.suptitle(\"Coffee Trade: Top Exporters vs Top Importers, stacked by decade\")\n",
    "plt.show()\n"
   ]
  },

    "prod_year_cols = df_production.drop(columns=[\"Country\", \"Coffee type\", \"Total_production\"]).columns\n",
    "by_type = (df_production.groupby(\"Coffee type\")[prod_year_cols].sum()\n",
    "           .sum(axis=1).sort_values(ascending=False))\n",
    "\n",
    "fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(8, 6))\n",
    "ax.pie(by_type, labels=by_type.index, autopct=\"%1.1f%%\", startangle=90,\n",
    "       colors=[\"#6F4E37\", \"#A9744F\", \"#D2B48C\"],\n",
    "       wedgeprops=dict(width=0.45, edgecolor=\"white\"),\n",
    "       pctdistance=0.78)\n",
    "ax.text(0, 0, \"Global\\nProduction\\nShare\", ha=\"center\", va=\"center\", fontsize=11)\n",
    "ax.set_title(\"Global Coffee Production by Type (1990/91–2019/20)\")\n",
    "plt.show()\n"
   ]

    "## Executive Summary\n",
    "Global exports have grown massively over the past two decades. When looking at the broader landscape, the biggest growth has been seen in Brazil, Viet Nam, the United States, and Germany. Brazil has seen the largest shift year-over-year growth. In 2019, Brazil was lagging behind Colombia before pulling ahead as one of the most successful exporters of coffee. Germany and the United States have remained the highest consumers of coffee. While the strongest players can be attractive, there are limitations to relying on them. \n",
    "At some point, there is a limit to the market for importers. In the case of a consumer market, a crowded market becomes more difficult to sell product. This usually means that time and money needs to be spent to appeal to niche markets. This strategy has higher risks because creating a new product for a niche market doesn't necessarily mean that the product will be profitable.\n",
    "As for reliance on major exporters and producers, this can be a mistake that reveals itself when there are disruptions in the supply chain. If trade disrupts a country that we rely on for a majority of our exports, then we will have to scramble to figure out where to get product. By diversifying our importers, we can continue to maintain a steady supply even when there are disruptions in the supply chain.\n",
    "With these considerations in mind, I suggest an investment across producers, exporters, and importers with year-over-year growth. In the case of exporters, Viet Nam, Indonesia, and India are the best choices. These exporters are seeing greater growth over time that could be greatly expanded. I do not recommend Colombia because it has decreased growth over the last few decades. New importers to focus on would include France, Italy, and Japan. These are new markets that are currently sustainable, but could see higher growth with more investment to find consumers."
